**F3-IMPL-002 (2026-08-10, Production Worker Process Contract, Deploy Lifecycle & Job Ownership Hardening, phase F3 — Production Hardening; second F3 implementation task, closes the worker/job-ownership gap F3-IMPL-001's own docstring in `backgroundJobsOwnership.ts` deliberately left open; dedicated worktree `E:\Ek Gelir\Siteler\DisKlinikCRM-worktrees\f3-impl-002`, branch `feature/f3-impl-002-production-worker-contract`, baseline `origin/main` @ `1909b186a01611c8be90313b7166085a887d05f4` — independently confirmed via `git fetch origin --prune`/`git rev-parse origin/main`, exact match to the expected baseline (PR #355/F3-IMPL-001 merge commit), no drift).** **Bounded 12-question pre-work inventory executed first**, classified `PARTIAL`: `server/src/worker.ts` already existed on this baseline and already worked correctly (`JobLock`-protected DB lease, unconditionally calls `startBackgroundJobs()`) — the actual gap was entirely deployment/process-management, not runtime code: no `ecosystem.config.*` anywhere in the repository (R-040), `scripts/noramedi-deploy.sh` only ever reloaded a `noramedi-api` PM2 process, never `noramedi-worker` (R-033), and the API's job-ownership default (owns jobs unless `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS=false`) could not safely be flipped without a repository-defined worker deploy lifecycle to back it — exactly the coupling F3-IMPL-001's own docstring named as its reason for not changing that default. Production itself (per `PRODUCTION_TOPOLOGY.md`'s `VERIFIED_PRODUCTION_OBSERVED` evidence) already runs a `noramedi-worker` PM2 process today, but that fact was not reproducible or verifiable from the repository. **Three coupled changes delivered in this one PR, per the task's own instruction not to silently alter production job ownership otherwise:** (1) `ecosystem.config.cjs` (new, repository root) — the single repository-defined PM2 process contract for both `noramedi-api` and `noramedi-worker` (`cwd`/`script`/`args` reusing the already-documented `npm run start`/`npm run start:worker` entrypoints, no new invocation shape), with the api app explicitly setting `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS=false` now that the worker's own lifecycle is itself repository-managed by this same file — no secrets added, only two small non-secret env vars per app. (2) `scripts/noramedi-deploy.sh` — steps 5-6 now reload/start **both** processes via `pm2 startOrReload ecosystem.config.cjs --only <name> --update-env` (self-healing: starts if missing, reloads if already running); a new step 8 verifies the worker reaches PM2 `online` status via a Node-parsed `pm2 jlist` check (no HTTP endpoint added to the worker — PM2 status only, consistent with `LAUNCH_GATES.md`'s own stated acceptance of that signal for worker liveness), aborting the whole deploy (`exit 1`) if it does not — a worker that fails to start is no longer silently ignored. (3) `server/src/utils/processRole.ts` (new) — an optional, validated `NORAMEDI_PROCESS_ROLE` env var (`api`/`worker`) asserted at startup by both `index.ts` and `worker.ts` immediately after `dotenv.config()`; unset is fully backward compatible (every existing dev/test/pre-existing-deployment environment behaves identically), while an unrecognized or entrypoint-mismatched declared value **fails closed** (throws before any job registration or port bind) rather than risking an ambiguous production role. `resolveWorkerBackgroundJobsOwnership()` added to `backgroundJobsOwnership.ts` alongside the pre-existing, byte-for-byte-unmodified `resolveApiBackgroundJobsOwnership()`, so the intended production ownership matrix (worker owns unconditionally, API does not once `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS=false` is set, no duplicate registration via the existing `JobLock` backstop, no zero-owner state given the new deploy-time worker verification) is proven by tests rather than read by eye. **No schema/migration change; no HTTP surface added to the worker.** **Tests:** `cd server && npm run typecheck` exit `0`; new `processRole.test.ts` (8/8); `backgroundJobsOwnership.test.ts` extended (10/10, was 7/7); `test:database-url-validation` (11/11) and `test:request-id-correlation` (2/2) re-run as unaffected regression checks; full `server:test:non-disposable` (69 members after wiring `test:process-role` in) — exit `0`, every member `&&`-chained so the aggregate's own exit code is proof of a full pass; `bash -n scripts/noramedi-deploy.sh` syntax-clean; `npm run test:runtime:postgres -- --summary-file=postgres-run-summary.json` (Docker-provisioned disposable Postgres) — first attempt failed transiently at the `prisma generate` step under concurrent host load from other background processes in this same session (not a code defect — `cd server && npm run typecheck`, which also runs `npx prisma generate`, had already succeeded standalone), immediately retried with no other background load and passed cleanly: `server:test:disposable-db` (25 members) exit `0`, `72/72` migrations applied, Docker cleanup succeeded, no orphaned container. **Security/tenant/KVKK impact: none negative** — no query/authorization/tenant-scoping path touched; no patient/clinic/message payload logged by any new code; the only new log lines are process-role/job-ownership startup diagnostics and PM2-status words, none of which can carry secrets/`DATABASE_URL`/PII. `RISK_REGISTER.md` R-033/R-040 rows updated with this repository-level mitigation's evidence pointer, R-034's duplicate-registration exposure reduced — **none closed**; all three remain `OPEN` pending an actual production deployment and verification, which this task explicitly did not perform. Files changed: `ecosystem.config.cjs` (new), `server/src/utils/processRole.ts` (new), `server/src/tests/processRole.test.ts` (new), `server/src/utils/backgroundJobsOwnership.ts`, `server/src/tests/backgroundJobsOwnership.test.ts`, `server/src/index.ts`, `server/src/worker.ts`, `scripts/noramedi-deploy.sh`, `server/package.json` (+1 test script, 2 aggregate-chain wirings), this file, `NORAMEDI_MASTER_TRACKER.md`, `phases/F3_PRODUCTION_HARDENING.md`, `evidence/README.md`, `RISK_REGISTER.md`, new evidence file [evidence/F3-IMPL-002_PRODUCTION_WORKER_PROCESS_CONTRACT.md](evidence/F3-IMPL-002_PRODUCTION_WORKER_PROCESS_CONTRACT.md). **F3 exit gate still NOT satisfied** — unchanged from F3-IMPL-001 (no live observability dashboard/alarm evidence, no full security-hardening checklist sign-off, no incident-response drill evidence); this task closed a distinct, bounded worker/deploy-lifecycle gap only. **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED`/`TESTS_PASSED`/`PR_OPENED`** — `NOT_MERGED`/`NOT_DEPLOYED`/`NOT_PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`. **Exact next task:** program-owner review/merge decision for this PR; independently, once merged and operationally deployed, the first real production deploy under `ecosystem.config.cjs` should be watched closely (may restart rather than gracefully reload both processes the first time — documented in the file itself), and a dedicated production-verification task should confirm `NORAMEDI_PROCESS_ROLE`/`RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS` actually resolve as intended on the live VPS before R-033/R-034/R-040 can be closed; the observability/security-hardening-checklist/incident-response-drill items F3-IMPL-001 already named remain the other open F3 exit-gate work, unaffected by this task.**

**F3-IMPL-001 (2026-08-10, First-Customer Production Hardening Baseline and Critical Controls, phase F3 — Production Hardening; first F3 implementation task, assigned externally by task brief citing F2-DOC-005's `F2_IMPLEMENTATION_EXIT_GATE = SATISFIED` immediately below — F2-DOC-005 itself explicitly states it does not auto-start F3, so this task's own assignment is the authorization, not this file; dedicated worktree `E:\Ek Gelir\Siteler\DisKlinikCRM-worktrees\f3-impl-001`, branch `feature/f3-impl-001-first-customer-production-hardening`, baseline `origin/main` @ `9cb256856b628a5ed5cc6ff85f84ec01a4a12cf0` — independently confirmed via `git fetch origin --prune`/`git rev-parse origin/main`, exact match to the expected baseline, the F2-DOC-005/PR #354 merge commit, no drift).** **Note on this file's own staleness:** the "Aktif faz"/"Aktif F1 görevi" sections further down this document (and their "Tarih ve güncelleme geçmişi" table) were never updated past the F0/F1 era (last dated entry 2026-07-21) despite F2 having since been fully implemented and exited (per the F2-DOC-005 entry immediately below and `NORAMEDI_MASTER_TRACKER.md` §4) — this is pre-existing documentation debt, not introduced by this task, and reconciling it is out of this task's bounded F3-hardening scope; flagged here for a future documentation task, consistent with this file's own convention of prepending current-state capsules rather than silently rewriting stale sections. **Bounded pre-work inventory executed first** (items A–O: request/correlation id, structured logging, error tracking, secret fail-hard, API/worker process separation, `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS` behavior, worker deploy/reload lifecycle, PII/PHI log redaction, platform-admin audit trail, security headers, rate limiting, startup/readiness behavior, health endpoints, Node/runtime contract, first-customer smoke support), each classified with file:line repository evidence — full matrix in the new evidence file. **Five bounded, coupled P0 gaps selected and closed, nothing else touched:** (1) `DATABASE_URL` fail-hard in production (`server/src/utils/databaseUrl.ts`, wired into `db.ts`) — closes a real silent-fallback risk (node-postgres falls back to `PG*` environment variables / driver defaults, e.g. localhost, when `connectionString` is `undefined`, which the prior `process.env.DATABASE_URL!` non-null assertion did nothing to prevent); **scoped to production only, not unconditional** — an unconditional version was tried first and empirically found (by actually running `server:test:non-disposable`, not assumed) to crash ~68 unrelated tests that import `db.ts` only transitively and never issue a real query, since `db.ts` constructs its Prisma client eagerly at import time; reverted to mirror the existing `ENCRYPTION_KEY` production-only pattern, preserving today's exact non-production behavior. (2) API/worker background-jobs ownership (`server/src/utils/backgroundJobsOwnership.ts`) — extracts the previously inline, untested `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS !== 'false'` decision into an explicit, unit-tested function, with a startup diagnostic logged in **both** `index.ts` and `worker.ts` so an operator can positively confirm from logs which process owns jobs (closing the `LAUNCH_GATES.md`-flagged "production's actual `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS` value is unverified" gap) — **the underlying default is deliberately NOT flipped**: `scripts/noramedi-deploy.sh` reloads only a `noramedi-api` PM2 process and references no dedicated worker process anywhere in deploy tooling, so making the API opt out of jobs by default (the fail-safe shape the task brief otherwise preferred) would silently stop production background jobs (reminders, data-retention cleanup, etc.) unless an operator has already, unverifiably, set `RUN_BACKGROUND_JOBS=true` — an unacceptable silent behavior change for a live pilot; deferred to a follow-up once a dedicated worker process is actually part of the deployment topology and can be verified in production. (3) `X-Request-Id` response header (`server/src/middleware/requestId.ts`) — echoes pino-http's per-request `req.id` (a per-process monotonic counter, not derived from any client-supplied header, so safe to expose) back to the client, closing the gap where a failed request's correlation id existed only in server-side logs. (4) Removed the triggering admin's email from 7 `console.log` call sites in `routes/platformAdmin.ts` (R-018), replacing it with the admin id. (5) Added a durable `PlatformAdminAuditEvent` audit trail (via a new standalone `writePlatformAdminAuditEvent` helper in `services/platformAdminAudit.ts`, reusing the existing `PlatformAdminAuditEvent` model/pattern) to `POST /backups/run` and `POST /backups/restore-test` (R-019) — previously these two privileged operational triggers had zero durable audit evidence, only the now-removed `console.log`. **R-018/R-019 are explicitly reduced, not closed** — of ~20 platform-admin privileged-mutation endpoints identified in the inventory, only these 2 gained audit coverage (a bounded representative high-risk path, per the task brief's own instruction not to redesign the authorization model); the ~91 other non-test `console.*` call sites found repo-wide are untouched. **No schema/migration change.** **Tests:** `cd server && npm run typecheck` clean (exit 0); new `databaseUrlValidation.test.ts` (11/11), `backgroundJobsOwnership.test.ts` (7/7), `requestIdCorrelation.test.ts` (2/2) — all pure/no-DB, wired into `server:test:non-disposable`; new `platformBackupAudit.test.ts` (3/3, exercises the real route handler chain against a real disposable Postgres database, proving admin-id attribution and zero PII/email in the audit row on the restore-test failure path — the deterministic, side-effect-free path in this sandbox since `/root/noramedi-backups` doesn't exist here) — wired into `server:test:disposable-db`. Full `server:test:non-disposable` (68 members) re-run in full after the production-only fix — 0 failures. Full `npm run test:runtime:postgres` orchestrator run (`server:test:disposable-db`, Docker-provisioned digest-pinned `postgres:16-alpine`, 72/72 migrations applied) — see exact result below/in the evidence file. **F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001-POSTMERGE-CI-001 (2026-08-09, Post-Merge Main CI Failure Investigation/Remediation, phase F2 — Modular Boundaries and Public Contracts; dedicated physical worktree `fix/f2-stage3-postmerge-securityincident-ci`, baseline `origin/main` @ `6a248ea1c359b2e86f39f9fff0b1d8577357fc10` — the exact PR #344/`F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001-R1` merge commit, independently re-confirmed via `git fetch origin main`/`git rev-parse origin/main`, no drift; triggered by a real post-merge main CI failure, `ci-main-and-nightly` run `31313169502`, `ci-layers / Layer 5: full-suite/compatibility fail-safe (backend, legacy server:test DB-required members)`, `server/src/tests/securityIncident.test.ts` test "22. Repeated cross-tenant denial: one resource stays medium, multiple resources escalate to high" — observed `54 passed, 1 failed`, assertion `null == true`).** **Causality check against PR #344 performed first, not assumed:** PR #344's diff (`server/src/services/imaging/public.ts`, `server/src/services/privacy/orphanFileInspection.ts`, `server/src/services/privacy/patientAnonymization.ts`, `server/package.json` script additions, docs) has zero dependency-graph overlap with `securityDetectionRules.ts`/`securityIncidentService.ts`/`securitySignalService.ts` or `securityIncident.test.ts` — `grep` for security-incident symbols across every file PR #344 touched returns zero matches, and none of PR #344's five commits (`92f77f4`/`560acdd`/`ea2d0c0`/`39364f1`/`7f3157e`) appear in that test file's `git log`. **Not a regression caused by PR #344.** **CodeGraph scope, narrowed to exactly the failing suite and its direct runtime dependencies** (no full-repo scan): `securityIncident.test.ts`, `securityDetectionRules.ts` (`evaluateCrossTenantDenialSignal`, the shared `safely()` fire-and-forget wrapper used by all 7 `evaluate*Signal` rule exports), `securityIncidentService.ts` (`upsertIncidentFromSignal`'s own multi-statement transaction: `findUnique` → `pg_advisory_xact_lock` → `findUnique` → `upsert` → conditional activity `create`), `securitySignalService.ts` (`recordSecuritySignal`/`countSignalsInWindow`). **Reproduction:** disposable PostgreSQL 16 provisioned locally (digest-pinned `postgres:16-alpine`, matching CI's own image), 72/72 migrations applied; `cd server && npm run test:security-incidents` run 7× under normal/throttled-DB conditions — **0/7 natural reproductions** (55 passed, 0 failed every run, 5–26s) — consistent with a load-dependent race, not a deterministic logic bug. A test-only, non-production-path artificial delay (`NMTEST_ARTIFICIAL_SIGNAL_DELAY_MS`, injected into `safely()`, removed before the real fix) reproduced the **exact** CI assertion (`assert.ok(singleResourceIncident)` on `null`) plus 3 sibling failures (tests 20/24/25 — every test whose fixed `sleep()` window was ≤200ms) **4/4 times**, isolating the mechanism precisely. **Root cause: Category C — pre-existing test-timing race (KVKK-CRIT-003 foundation, PR #167/#178, long before PR #344), not a threshold/escalation/dedup/tenant-isolation logic defect.** `evaluateCrossTenantDenialSignal` and its six siblings are deliberately fire-and-forget (`void safely(async () => {...})`, non-blocking by explicit design — regression-proven by test 21, "returns synchronously ... elapsed < 50ms"); the background work behind that `void` return is genuinely multi-round-trip (INSERT + COUNT + optional distinct-resource SELECT + the upsert transaction's own ~4–5 round trips). Eight of nine `sleep()` call sites in the test file (tests 20, 22, 23, 24, 25) observed this fire-and-forget work via a **fixed 150–200ms guess**, not a durable-completion barrier — whenever real round-trip latency (Docker/Postgres/Node scheduling under a busy CI runner) exceeded that guess, the test's read-back query ran before the incident row existed, producing exactly the reported `null == true`. The ninth `sleep(5)` call (dedup/concurrency section, separating two directly-`await`ed `upsertIncidentFromSignal` calls to prove `lastDetectedAt` advances) is unrelated and untouched. **Fix — determinism at the real boundary, no assertion weakened, no `sleep()` added:** `securityDetectionRules.ts`'s shared `safely()` wrapper now tracks its own in-flight promise in a module-level `Set` (`pendingDetectionWork`) and exports a new `flushPendingSecurityDetectionWork()` that awaits everything currently tracked; the test file's 8 racy `sleep()` calls (tests 20, 22, 23, 24, 25) now `await flushPendingSecurityDetectionWork()` instead. No `evaluate*Signal` export's signature, call site, or return timing changed — production callers still get an immediate, synchronous `void` return (test 21 still passes, including under the same adversarial delay used to prove the fix). This is additive bookkeeping only (`Set.add`/`Set.delete` around an already-existing `await`), invisible to every production caller. **Tests:** `cd server && npm run typecheck` clean; `npm run test:security-incidents` **55/55 passed, 0 failed**, repeated **6× post-fix** (3× normal conditions, 3× under the identical adversarial 250ms delay that deterministically broke the pre-fix code 4/4 times) — 0 failures in all 6 runs; `git diff --check` clean. `npm run test:runtime:postgres-compat -- --summary-file=postgres-compat-run-summary.json` (the exact CI Layer 5 backend command) reached 116 passed before aborting on an **unrelated, pre-existing, environment-specific** failure in `test:clinic-bulk-export` ("status DTO never serializes sensitive fields") — a Windows-local `core.autocrlf=true` CRLF-checkout artifact (the test does an exact-byte `\n`-embedded string match against `routes/clinicBulkExport.ts`, which this Windows worktree checks out with CRLF line endings; `git diff` for this task touches only 2 files, neither this one), out of scope, not caused by this task or PR #344, not expected on the Linux `ubuntu-latest` CI runners this repository's workflows actually use — this aborted the `&&` chain before it reached `test:security-incidents` later in the same aggregate, so the isolated, repeated direct runs above are this task's primary evidence for the fixed test. `npm run test:runtime:postgres -- --summary-file=postgres-run-summary.json` (`server:test:disposable-db`, does not include `test:security-incidents`/`test:clinic-bulk-export`) run as a general regression safety net per the mandated validation command list — see `postgres-run-summary.json` at repo root for its exact result. Files changed: `server/src/services/security/securityDetectionRules.ts`, `server/src/tests/securityIncident.test.ts`, this file, `NORAMEDI_MASTER_TRACKER.md`, `phases/F2_MODULAR_BOUNDARIES.md`, `evidence/README.md`, new evidence file. No schema/migration/route file touched. Full evidence: [evidence/F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001-POSTMERGE-CI-001_SECURITY_INCIDENT_MAIN_CI_REMEDIATION.md](evidence/F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001-POSTMERGE-CI-001_SECURITY_INCIDENT_MAIN_CI_REMEDIATION.md). **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED`** ([PR #345](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/pull/345), head `9a2a7a63617682a0a3d248a8c4299a42ecf609a3` — includes a reconciliation merge against `origin/main` @ `53c2aba1...` after PR #343 merged mid-task, doc-only conflict, zero `server/` files touched by the reconciliation) — `NOT_MERGED` / `NOT_DEPLOYED` / `NOT_PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`. **Does not touch, revisit, or invalidate `F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001`/`F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001-R1`'s own imaging-migration conclusions** (below) — this is an unrelated, orthogonal CI blocker in a different domain (security-incident detection), discovered in the same post-merge CI cycle. **Exact next task:** program-owner review/merge decision for this PR; separately, the unrelated Windows-CRLF `test:clinic-bulk-export` false-failure observed in §6 of the evidence doc is not CI-blocking (Linux runners preserve LF) but may warrant a `.gitattributes` hardening follow-up if Windows-local full-chain reproduction is expected to remain supported.**

**F2-CT-32-R2 (2026-08-08, ImagingRequest PATCH/Cancel Residual Race Remediation, phase F2 — Modular Boundaries and Public Contracts; branch `fix/f2-ct-32-r2-imaging-request-residual-race`, baseline `origin/main` @ `51e102d19a5ddda87a49ad0367172b3eaae05d09` — confirmed unchanged at implementation time; BLOCKER/URGENT, opened by a post-merge main CI failure on `ci-main-and-nightly` run `31276844302`, "Layer 3: disposable PostgreSQL tests", `server/src/tests/imagingRequestConcurrencyCharacterization.test.ts` — CT-32 148 passed / 6 failed, round-1 signature `PATCH=200, cancel=200, final=cancelled`, aggregate `EXACTLY_ONE_WINNER=29/30`, `BOTH_SUCCESS_SILENT_CLOBBER=1/30`).** Independently reproduced the CI failure deterministically (not accepted as flake): 0/600 natural local reproductions against a low-latency disposable Postgres (cancel structurally faster than PATCH every round — no `validateClinicalLinks` round-trips — so it wins the CAS race 100% of the time locally), but a forced-interleaving test-only hook (`installImagingRequestRaceTestHooks`, new, mirrors `patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts`'s established pattern) forcing cancel's read to begin only after PATCH's compare-and-set write had already committed reproduced the exact CI signature on demand, every time. **Root cause:** the F2-CT-32-R1 CAS guard (merged, not regressed) is airtight for any writer whose own read is stale at write time, but CT-32 races a *chainable* pair (`scheduled` is itself a valid predecessor of `cancelled`) — when PATCH's entire operation completes before cancel's own read executes, cancel legitimately observes `scheduled` and its own CAS legitimately commits `scheduled -> cancelled`; both responses are individually honest, the pair still violates the required invariant. `imagingRequestConcurrencyGuard.test.ts`'s own header comment for scenario A had already flagged exactly this chainable-pair ambiguity as something to design around, not accept — CT-32 uses the ambiguous pair that suite's author deliberately avoided. Matches, and is resolved by, the already-accepted precedent in `patientEmergencyContactsConcurrency.ts` (F1-004-P1-R2-R3): once a competing transaction has already committed before this transaction's own first read executes, the two are — from PostgreSQL's own point of view — genuinely, unambiguously sequential; no signal visible only inside the second transaction can distinguish that from a deliberate sequential call. **Fix:** additive, optional, backward-compatible `expectedStatus` precondition field on both `PATCH /imaging/requests/:id` (existing body) and `PATCH /imaging/requests/:id/cancel` (new optional body) — the caller's own pre-request belief about the row's status, immune to server-side read timing by construction (same technique as that file's own `expectedCurrentPrimaryContactId` token-protected mode). Omitted, behavior is byte-for-byte F2-CT-32-R1 (including its now-documented residual gap for callers that don't opt in — proven as a deliberate characterization, not silently widened, in the new regression test's section 1). No schema/migration — confirmed not needed, not made. **Tests:** `typecheck` clean; CT-32 revised (not deleted, per its own established convention) to supply `expectedStatus`, re-run at `CT32_ROUNDS=100`: **504/504**, `EXACTLY_ONE_WINNER` 100/100, clobber 0/100; new `imagingRequestConcurrencyForcedInterleaving.test.ts` (**55/55**) proves, under the identical forced schedule that broke legacy mode: legacy (no `expectedStatus`) still reproduces `BOTH_SUCCESS` deterministically (a characterized, accepted limitation) while the fix makes it impossible every round, plus sequential-PATCH-then-cancel (E), sequential-cancel-then-PATCH-rejected (F), two-row independence (G), and cross-tenant unchanged (H); `imagingRequestConcurrencyGuard.test.ts` re-verified **73/73**, no regression; `test:imaging` **103/103**; `test:imaging-lifecycle-facade` **34/34**; `test:imaging-study-request-patient-consistency` **12/12**; `test:imaging-study-request-patient-mismatch-detector` **7/7**; `guardrail:test` **74/74**; `guardrail:scan` exit 0; `git diff --check` clean; full `npm run test:runtime:postgres` orchestrator run (the exact command CI Layer 3 runs) against disposable PostgreSQL 16 (`postgres:16-alpine`, digest-pinned) — `outcome.exitCode: 0`, `"tests passed"`, `"cleanup succeeded"`. Full evidence: [evidence/F2-CT-32-R2_IMAGING_REQUEST_RESIDUAL_RACE_REMEDIATION.md](evidence/F2-CT-32-R2_IMAGING_REQUEST_RESIDUAL_RACE_REMEDIATION.md). **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED`** — `PR_OPENED` once opened (this task explicitly does not merge), `NOT_MERGED` / `NOT_DEPLOYED` / `NOT_PRODUCTION_VERIFIED`. **`F2-STAGE3-AUTH-001`'s merged authorization decision is NOT revoked** — nothing found here invalidates that authorization's own assumptions. **`F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001` remains explicitly BLOCKED** until this PR merges and exact post-merge `main` CI is independently re-verified green — do not claim otherwise until that is done. **Exact next task:** open the PR against `main` (do not merge), then await CI + program-controller review; only after merge + green post-merge CI may `F2-STAGE3-IMPL-001` resume.**

Sample/metrics regenerated deterministically (2×+ reruns, byte-identical); 6 new regression tests (`val004Determinism.test.js`, now 50/50). **F2-ADR-ORG-DASH-002 (2026-08-06, Resolve organizationDashboard Domain Ownership Ambiguity, phase F2 — Modular Monolith Boundary Remediation / Guardrail Signal Quality; branch `docs/f2-adr-org-dashboard-002-ownership`, PR #331).** Resolves the **last** open item in the F2 guardrail-signal-quality chain: the dual-domain ownership ambiguity of `server/src/routes/organizationDashboard.ts`, deferred in turn by F2-GUARDRAIL-VAL-001 §9 (collision #4), F2-GUARDRAIL-VAL-002 §5.5, and F2-ADR-ORG-DASH-001 (each of which explicitly stated it was *not* resolving it). **Baseline:** `origin/main` @ `46acae8415020cb0bd340fbc854c4187c43e3662`, with PR #329 (`99436e5…`) and PR #328 (`46acae8…`, the tip) both re-verified `MERGED` via `gh pr view` and both confirmed ancestors of `origin/main`; post-merge `main` CI run `31098023681` `success`. The pre-created agent worktree sat one commit behind at `99436e5`, so the branch was created explicitly from `origin/main` — recorded because it would otherwise have produced a silently stale baseline.

**Decision (ADR [`ADR-F2-ORG-DASH-OWNERSHIP`](../architecture/f2/ADR-F2-ORG-DASH-OWNERSHIP.md), Option A, HIGH confidence): the file is owned by `core-org-clinic-membership`, and no code split is a prerequisite for that assignment.** The adjudication was made on authorization semantics, tenant-scope derivation, owned-model access, response identity, and accepted consumer ownership — never on the filename, and never to reduce finding counts. Decisive facts, all from direct source reads: the route's gate (`authorize(['OWNER','ORG_ADMIN'])` + `canAccessOrganizationDashboard()` = `isOrganizationAdmin()`) is byte-identical to `organizationBranches.ts`'s organization-administration gate and **strictly narrower** than the gate every `reports.ts` endpoint uses (`OWNER|ORG_ADMIN|CLINIC_MANAGER|BILLING`); the handler derives its own security boundary from `core-org-clinic-membership`-owned state (`Clinic.organizationId`, `Clinic.status`, `UserClinic`, `canAccessAllClinics`, `allowedClinicIds`), which a reporting-owned classification would turn into a cross-domain read *into the tenant-critical membership tables*; and its sole consumer `src/pages/OrganizationDashboard.tsx` is already accepted-listed under `core-org-clinic-membership` in both F0-003 and F2-PREP-001. An eight-block responsibility decomposition put 6 blocks unambiguously in ORG, 1 already relocated to `core-shared-platform-infrastructure` by merged PR #328, and 1 mixed — **no block resolved to `reporting-analytics`.** Option B (reporting-owned) and Option C (physical split — no clean seam; one handler, one composed response, would need four new contracts and risk response-shape drift) were rejected; **Option D (composition shell over public read contracts) is accepted as the target end-state but is not achievable today**, since none of the required contracts exists.

**Implementation (minimal, mechanical, inside the authorization boundary): one `domain-map.json` value, `UNRESOLVED` → `core-org-clinic-membership`, plus an additive `provenance.subsequentCorrections[]` note that supersedes VAL-002's entry without rewriting it.** Zero application source, Prisma schema, migration, route-path, response-shape, or authorization change. **Guardrail (4 `--deterministic` runs, byte-compared pairwise):** 1,039 → 1,039 total findings; 1,024/15 `NEW`/`EXISTING` unchanged; 0/0 errors/warnings and 0 skipped in all four; **0 tuples added, 0 removed, 0 finding IDs changed**; `callerDomain: UNRESOLVED` **5 → 0**; findings targeting `organizationDashboard.ts` **0 → 0**; high-risk-domain-touching **526 → 526, identical set by ID**; `resolvedBaselineEdgeIds` unchanged; guardrail baseline file untouched. **Exactly five finding labels changed — nothing suppressed.** Domain map now has **0 `UNRESOLVED` entries (247/247 mapped)** and **0 open F0-003 ownership collisions**. **Explicitly still open:** the handler's direct cross-domain Prisma reads of `Appointment`/`Patient`/`TreatmentCase`/`Payment` — this decision makes that debt *more* visible, not less, and the guardrail cannot see it in either case (`edgeExtraction.ts` is import-only). **Tests:** `typecheck`, `typecheck:guardrail`, `git diff --check` clean; `test:orgdash` 35/35; `guardrail:test` 74/74; `test:runtime:unit` 74/74; `test:finance` 25/25; `test:dashboard` 38/38; `test:roles` 142/142. **Deployment classification: docs/config-only, no application deployment required.** Blocking enforcement remains unauthorized and was not enabled. **F1-004-P1-R2-R3 (2026-08-05, Emergency Contact CREATE-Race Residual Limitation Closed via Optimistic-Concurrency Precondition Token, phase F1 — Engineering Safety Net and Concurrency Verification; same branch/PR as F1-004-P1-R2 below, follow-up task).** Same worktree/branch/PR (`fix/f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, PR #325), starting from R2's own head `85f7c52c5a63ff40d21c8261dbebe685138cdfbf`, baseline re-fetched `origin/main` @ `32adf919cb331ae2ce4f681bd37e857ded8065ab`. **Deterministically reproduced** (via real, production-embedded, no-op-unless-installed test synchronization hooks forcing request B's entire critical section to start after request A's commit) the CREATE-race CI failure R2's §15 recorded but left open (run [31020654709](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31020654709) attempt 1, `A=201 B=201`): R2's same-connection "prior vs current" comparison cannot detect a race where the loser's OWN first read is itself delayed past the winner's commit — both reads then observe identical, fully-committed state, formally indistinguishable from legitimate sequential replacement. 500 local (unforced) rounds reproduced this zero times — confirms the CI-observed rate is a genuine scheduling/latency artifact, not a local-repro gap. **Proved by elimination** that no further server-only signal (lock/read reordering, row-lock sentinel, SERIALIZABLE — re-confirmed still invalid per R2's cross-tenant false-positive finding, wall-clock heuristic — rejected for unprovable clock-skew dependence) can close this airtight, since once a transaction holds the lock, anything it reads is a fully-committed fact the database cannot tag with "when did the HTTP request arrive." Escalated per this task's own stop condition rather than decided unilaterally; **program owner selected an additive, optional, backward-compatible `expectedCurrentPrimaryContactId` precondition** on `POST`/`PUT` (omitted=legacy fallback, unchanged, knowingly not race-free; explicit `null`/a contact id=client's own pre-formed belief), enforced under the existing per-patient advisory lock as true optimistic-concurrency control — airtight regardless of scheduling, verified under the exact worst-case forced schedule (20/20 deterministic rounds) plus 500/500 token-protected CREATE and 500/500 token-protected UPDATE natural-race stress rounds, all correct. Legacy behavior's known limitation is preserved and permanently characterization-tested (asserts it still reproduces under forcing), never hidden. **Frontend updated** (`PatientEmergencyContactsPanel.tsx`/`PatientEmergencyContactForm.tsx`) to always send the precondition, including explicit `null`, for every create/replace-primary operation. **No migration** — `server/prisma/` untouched, confirmed via empty `git diff --stat`. **Tests:** `patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` 31/31 (+6); new `patientEmergencyContactsCreateRaceForcedInterleaving.test.ts` 7/7; `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts` 19/19 (legacy 100/150 unchanged + new token-protected 500/500 CREATE + 500/500 UPDATE); server + frontend typecheck clean; 40/40 frontend component tests (+4); full `test:runtime:postgres` disposable-DB profile run **5 independent successful process-level times**, zero residual Docker resources each time. **F1-004-P1-R2 (2026-08-05, Emergency Contact Update Race — Root-Cause Confirmation and Fix, phase F1 — Engineering Safety Net and Concurrency Verification; independent of, does not touch or block, the F2-GUARDRAIL-IMPL-001 entry below).** Fresh isolated worktree `D:\Mustafa\Siteler\DisKlinikCRM-worktrees\f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, branch `fix/f1-004-p1-r2-emergency-contact-update-race`, baseline freshly-fetched `origin/main` @ `9b10bc9fde0ceaeb58d90860a950143de7123910` (PR #323's own merge commit) — confirmed clean, no reused dirty state from the pre-existing unmerged `fix/f1-004-p1-emergency-contact-primary-update-concurrency` (PR #311, closed unmerged as redundant) or already-merged `hotfix/us-01-2-primary-update-race` (PR #310) worktrees. **Parent context:** main CI run [31002888303](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31002888303) attempt 1 failed `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts`'s concurrent-UPDATE scenario (`A=200, B=200`) on a commit that **already included** PR #310's advisory-lock fix; attempt 2 of the same run passed with no code change — not dismissed as flaky. **Root cause, deterministically reproduced via a forced-interleaving harness (temporary, deleted before this PR, never merged), not a correlation:** PR #310's "prior primary" optimistic-concurrency snapshot was read via a SEPARATE, unlocked Prisma query issued before its promoting transaction opened — an event independently racing the transaction's own connection checkout against the shared 10-connection pg pool (`server/src/db.ts`). Under contention, the losing request's prior-read could be delayed past the winning request's full commit, silently absorbing the winner's row as "prior truth." The database's own single-primary invariant was **never** violated (exactly one committed primary throughout) — only the "loser must get 409" HTTP contract was. **Alternative considered and rejected:** SERIALIZABLE isolation — a raw-SQL probe confirmed it correctly aborts genuine overlapping races (SQLSTATE `40001`), but it broke the suite's own tenant-isolation scenario (two unrelated patients/organizations must never interfere): on this small/sparse table Postgres's planner favors a sequential scan for the "current primary" read, so SERIALIZABLE's predicate locks land at page/table granularity, producing false-positive cross-tenant `409`s. **Fix:** both the "prior" and "current" reads around the existing per-patient `pg_advisory_xact_lock` now run on the SAME `prisma.$transaction` (same connection) — the only gap between them is the lock-wait itself, never a second independently-poolable database round trip. Default (READ COMMITTED) isolation. **No migration** — the existing partial unique index remains the unchanged backstop. **R2 review-remediation update (2026-08-05, same PR, commit `b01ea47124584f918351afd6feb02e6f46e4839a`, addresses 2/2 PR #325 review threads, now resolved):** (1) `isPrimaryContactConflict()` no longer maps a generic Prisma `P2034` (deadlock/serialization failure) to `PRIMARY_CONTACT_CONFLICT` — a bare `P2034` is not inherently a primary-contact-specific signal and now falls through to the route's existing generic 500 handling; `PrimaryContactConflictError` and `P2002` (the two proven primary-contact-specific signals) are unchanged; (2) added `PATIENT_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_{CREATE,UPDATE}_RACE_ROUNDS` env overrides to the concurrency suite for local diagnostic runs only — strictly validated (bounded positive integer, never silently coerced/clamped), CI never sets them so CI always exercises the full committed defaults (100/150) regardless. **Tests (current, post-R2):** `patientEmergencyContacts.test.ts` 25/25 (net +1 vs. R1's 24/24: removed the now-incorrect P2034-is-a-conflict unit test, added 2 covering the corrected non-classification); `patientEmergencyContactsPrimaryConcurrency.test.ts` 17/17 (R1's 8 concurrency/stress scenarios + 9 new stress-round-configuration-validation unit tests — CREATE-race stress 100/100, UPDATE-race stress 150/150, unchanged); server typecheck clean; root `test:runtime:unit` 74/74; full `test:runtime:postgres` disposable-DB profile (16 chained scripts) run **3 independent successful process-level times against this exact post-R2 commit**, exit `0` each, zero residual Docker resources each time. Tenant/clinic scope re-verified unchanged; no route-level authorization code touched. **Residual limitation, documented honestly:** no purely server-side mechanism can formally guarantee a conflict signal when a request's ENTIRE database interaction happens to be scheduled strictly after a competing request's full commit — closing that fully would require a client-supplied concurrency token (changes API semantics) or a global lock (forbidden); this fix closes the specific, proven mechanism behind the observed CI failure, empirically verified across 250 stress rounds over 3 independent runs. Full evidence: [evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md](evidence/F1-004-P1-R2_EMERGENCY_CONTACT_UPDATE_RACE_ROOT_CAUSE_AND_FIX.md) + JSON companion. No F2-guardrail/F2-SEC validation change included. **PR [#325](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/pull/325)**, reconciled against `origin/main` (post-PR #324) in this same task, commit `fd1148f8341311baee3db1b2a7313522d7ad2404` — **not merged, not deployed, not production-verified.** **CI on the reconciled head, first real-world observation of the documented residual limitation (see evidence §15/JSON `firstRealCiObservationOfResidualLimitation`):** first attempt (run [31020654709](https://github.com/MustafaBasol/DisKlinikCRM/actions/runs/31020654709)) FAILED — `Layer 3: disposable PostgreSQL tests`, CREATE-race stress round 0, both concurrent creates got `201` instead of one `201`+one `409` — the residual gap this fix's own evidence doc already names as theoretically unclosable (a request's entire DB interaction scheduled strictly after a competitor's full commit), never before observed in a real CI run; reproduced 0/300 times locally across 3 independent disposable-DB runs on this exact commit. A diagnostic rerun of only the failed job (`gh run rerun --failed`, no code/test change) then passed, and the overall run is `success`. **Task status: `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_REVIEW`, CI green on rerun, program-owner review of the residual-limitation observation recommended before merge (not merely a review formality).** Merge safety: narrowly scoped, all tests pass, no migration — the one open question is the acceptable real-world frequency of the residual race just observed, a program-owner decision, not decided by this task. **F2-GUARDRAIL-IMPL-001 (2026-08-05, Exact-Edge Architecture Guardrail, Report-Only Baseline — implementation, repository-only/report-only/non-blocking only; isolated worktree/branch `feature/f2-guardrail-impl-001-report-only-exact-edge`, freshly-fetched `origin/main` @ `da23f6fd4b86d4a37dc56f90859b0806f8ce60c6` — PR #321's own merge commit — no drift at task start; independently re-confirmed via `gh pr view 321`, `gh run view 30996305595` (`ci-main-and-nightly`, `success`), and `git merge-base --is-ancestor`).** Implements the guardrail F2-SEC-003 §6 named but did not build. Design: a small TypeScript-compiler-API CLI under `scripts/architecture-guardrail/` (`lib/` split into `configLoader`/`sourceDiscovery`/`moduleResolution`/`edgeExtraction`/`classification`/`normalization`/`globMatch`/`findingId`/`baseline`/`redact`/`report`, plus `cli.ts` orchestration), following the existing `scripts/test-runtime/` convention (own `tsconfig.json`, `tsx`-run, hand-rolled test harness, no new test-framework dependency). **No new npm dependency** — uses the already-installed root TypeScript compiler API (syntactic `ts.createSourceFile` parse, not regex, per the task's own instruction); `package-lock.json` unchanged. Scans exactly the five roots `F2-GUARDRAIL-PREP-010-A` itself scoped (`server/src/{routes,services,jobs,middleware,utils}`), extracting cross-domain relative-import edges into the unchanged frozen tuple `(callerPath, callerSymbol, ownerDomain, targetModelOrSymbol, accessKind)`; domain ownership resolved via a checked-in map (`config/domain-map.json`, 179 entries) mechanically derived from the already-accepted `F0-003_module_ownership_inventory.json`, restricted to those five roots (4 files F0-003 itself lists under more than one domain are mapped to `UNRESOLVED`, never guessed). **No new baseline artifact is committed** — comparison consumes the existing `F2-GUARDRAIL-PREP-010-A` 71-edge JSON directly, read-only, matched on `(ownerDomain, targetModelOrSymbol, accessKind, callerPath exact-or-glob)` — `callerSymbol` is deliberately excluded from the match key and documented as such (most of the 71 baseline entries store it as descriptive prose, not an exact identifier; only the 6 `-R1`-corrected `CDA-067..072` edges do). Exit-code contract: 0 regardless of finding count, non-zero only for a genuine config/tool failure (malformed/unreadable config or baseline, or a configured scan root missing on disk) — tested both as a pure function and a real spawned subprocess. Deterministic: byte-identical output verified across two independent runs over the same commit. No absolute path, and no secret-like pattern (using the exact regex `ci-layers.yml`'s own disposable-runtime jobs already use), appears anywhere in a generated report — both independently verified. **56/56 focused unit tests pass** (exact-edge extraction, callerPath/callerSymbol/ownerDomain/targetModelOrSymbol/accessKind classification, deterministic IDs/sorting, no-absolute-paths, empty/multiple findings, malformed config/baseline, unreadable/missing configured path, both exit-code branches, JSON shape, secret redaction, Windows/POSIX normalization). Root frontend build, server typecheck (Prisma generate + `tsc --noEmit`), and the pre-existing `scripts/test-runtime` typecheck/unit-test jobs were re-run unaffected (all exit 0; 74/74 pre-existing orchestrator unit tests still pass) to confirm no regression. `actionlint v1.7.12` (same pinned version `workflow-and-syntax-lint` uses) clean against the full `.github/workflows/**` tree. **Real-scope scan result on `origin/main` @ `da23f6f`: 247 files discovered/parsed, 0 skipped, 1,031 findings (1,018 `NEW` / 13 `EXISTING`), 0 errors, 0 warnings, exit 0.** The high `NEW` count is expected and explicitly documented, not claimed as 1,031 real violations — this guardrail is exhaustive (one finding per imported binding per call site) while the 71-edge baseline is a curated, grouped manual sample; closing that gap is exactly the false-positive-validation prerequisite F2-SEC-003 §5.2 names before any CI-blocking use, which this task does not attempt. CI integration: one new standalone Layer 1 job, `architecture-guardrail-report-only`, added to `.github/workflows/ci-layers.yml` (SHA-pinned actions matching every existing job; JSON-validates and secret-scans its own report; uploads it as an artifact) — deliberately **not** added to any other job's `needs:` list and nothing needs it, so a guardrail-tool failure can never gate application tests; no existing job, `needs:` list, or path coverage was narrowed, removed, or weakened; `continue-on-error` is not used, so a genuine tool crash still turns only this new job red. Every generated report carries an explicit tenant-scope disclaimer (advisory signal only; absence of findings is not proof of tenant isolation; runtime tenant tests/authorization tests/ORM guards/future RLS remain independent controls; raw SQL and dynamic Prisma access may need explicit human review) — no tenant filter was read, changed, or relied upon for any correctness claim. **This task does not authorize CI-blocking enforcement, does not commit a self-referential baseline, and does not claim any tenant-isolation proof** — production enforcement remains gated on F2-SEC-003 §5.2's own prerequisites (program-owner false-positive review; F2-SEC-001/F2-SEC-002 deployment + production verification), neither performed by this task. **Discrepancy note:** the assigning brief's authoritative-sources list cited `docs/program/evidence/F2-GUARDRAIL-PREP-011*`; no such file exists on `origin/main` — recorded as a factual correction (F2-SEC-003 supersedes and independently supplies full authorization; not a stop condition). **Security task-ID normalization (no conflicting/reversed/duplicated reference found):** **F2-SEC-001 — Enforce Clinic Membership on Instagram Inbox Status Mutation.** **F2-SEC-002 — Remove Global Default-Clinic Resolution from Legacy WhatsApp Public API.** Grepped for both IDs plus "Instagram"/"WhatsApp" across PR #315's two changed files — every occurrence agrees (SEC-001↔Instagram, SEC-002↔WhatsApp), zero reversed/duplicate/ambiguous references found. **Both independently re-verified as currently present on `origin/main` @ `c731e7a`, by direct source read (not merely cited from the evidence branch):** `server/src/routes/instagramInbox.ts`'s `PATCH /instagram/inbox/:id/status` scopes its `findFirst`/`update` only by `organizationId`, with no `allowedClinicIds`/`getAccessibleClinicIds` clinic-membership check — unlike the same file's sibling `/assign-clinic` handler, which does check it — permitting a same-organization, cross-clinic status mutation plus full unredacted row disclosure (`lastMessageText`, `senderUsername`, `externalSenderId`, `patientId`, no `select`). `server/src/routes/whatsapp.ts`'s `getDefaultClinic()` (`prisma.clinic.findFirst({ orderBy: { createdAt: 'asc' } })`) is called directly by all 6 routes mounted at `/api/public/whatsapp` (`server/src/index.ts:183`), gated only by a single global `WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — no clinic/org identity resolution at all. **Both remain unresolved on current `main`; neither is touched, mitigated, or fixed by this task or by any of the three evidence PRs (all are documentation-only).**

**Exact-edge tuple semantics (frozen by PR #313, reconciled, not invented):** identity = `(callerPath, callerSymbol, ownerDomain, targetModelOrSymbol, accessKind)`; non-identity metadata (`classification`, `justificationEvidenceId`, `expiryOrRemovalTask`, optional `callerPathGlob`) never participates in matching. PR #313's own `frozenEdgeEnforcementSemantics` rule 6 explicitly forecloses broadening: a `callerPathGlob` match "does NOT automatically authorize additional call sites beyond the exact `callerPath` values it was constructed from — glob presence is a grouping convenience, never a grant of authority over files not yet evidenced"; a new symbol, file, target, or access-kind is each its own new edge requiring its own review (rules 2–5). This is accepted here as the frozen identity — no field beyond these five is added or implied.

**Legacy allowlist decision (recorded explicitly, per PR #313's own self-classification, adopted unchanged):** the `LEGACY_ALLOWLISTED_DIRECT_ACCESS` bucket (36 of 71 edges) is **descriptive evidence of current tolerated dependencies only** — it is **not** an accepted target architecture, **not** a permanent permission model, **not** authorization for any new cross-domain edge, and **not** permission to broaden an existing edge's tuple (path-only/domain-only/wildcard matching is explicitly excluded, see above). It is subject to exact-edge freezing (identity as defined above) and incremental retirement. `PROHIBITED_NEW_PATTERN` (10) and `UNRESOLVED_OWNERSHIP` (5) are separately, explicitly **not** blessed by their mere presence in this inventory.

**Canonical tenant-scoped mutation-contract gap (recorded, not closed):** **no generally accepted canonical tenant-scoped mutation contract currently exists anywhere in this repository.** PR #314's own document is explicit and is adopted unchanged: all 3 `SELECTED_REFERENCE_BASE` candidates remain reference bases only, each independently marked `HARDENING_REQUIRED_BEFORE_CANONICAL_ADOPTION` and `NOT_APPROVED_AS_CANONICAL_CONTRACT`; its own validation gate greps for and forbids phrases like "ready reference contract" or "canonical (scoped) mutation contract exists." **No candidate — including CAND-07 below — may be copied into new code as "the canonical pattern" as of this consolidation.**

PR #314's own §11 already independently discovered, mid-review, that PR #304 had since moved to head `7bcd1c8...` (F2-IMPL-001-A-R2) adding explicit leading `clinicId` parameters to all four methods, closing Finding 1 — but explicitly declined to reclassify CAND-07 on that basis, flagging the question open for "a PREP-010-C-R2 or program-owner decision." **This consolidation is that decision.** PR #304 has since additionally reached R3 (merged, `c731e7a`), closing a second, independent gap PR #314 never analyzed (the legal-hold TOCTOU race, closed via the atomic `{ id, clinicId, study: { clinicId, legalHold: false } }` predicate). **Outcome: `STRENGTHENED_REFERENCE_BASE`** — not `unchanged` (Finding 1, CAND-07's own stated rejection basis, is conclusively closed on merged `main`), not `partially superseded` (nothing supersedes it), not `no longer relevant` (it is now the most rigorously tenant-scoped mutation pattern in the repository). **Not promoted to canonical** — the facade remains additive/unused (zero production callers, so no real caller-integration evidence exists), and retains a public test-seam export (`__setImagingStorageExistenceCheckerForTest`, `__setRedactionPreMutationBarrierForTest`) that CAND-07's original rejection separately cited as an anti-pattern category and that this consolidation does not re-adjudicate. Formal reclassification of CAND-07 inside PR #314's own evidence document is left to that PR's own author/a future correction pass — this consolidation records the finding in the shared program-control documents only, per its own scope restriction (no other branch's files are touched by this task).

**PR #313/#315/#314 merge-order recommendation (no PR merged by this task):** since none of the three touches a shared program-control document and their new-file sets are mutually disjoint, no git conflict is expected in any order. Recommended sequence: **(1)** this consolidation PR merges first, so the security gate below, the legacy-allowlist caveats, the canonical-gap statement, and the CAND-07 finding are on `main` before any evidence PR's prose could be read in isolation as already-accepted architecture; **(2)** PR #315 next (documents the two live security blockers — highest urgency); **(3)** PR #313 next (freezes the 71-edge baseline any future guardrail tooling would key off); **(4)** PR #314 last, ideally after a lightweight correction pass that formally reclassifies CAND-07 per this consolidation's finding (not required for merge). No PR is recommended to supersede another — all three are additive, non-overlapping evidence and complement, not duplicate, one another.

**Guardrail implementation authorization decision: CI guardrail implementation (lint rule, dependency-cruiser enforcement, or any CI boundary check) is NOT authorized.** F2-SEC-001 and F2-SEC-002 remain open, live, unpatched security defects on current `main` (independently re-confirmed above). Prior update: 2026-08-03 (**F2-IMPL-001-A-R3 — Atomic Legal-Hold Enforcement and Final CI Reconciliation (continued on the existing open PR #304 branch `feature/f2-impl-001-a-unused-imaging-lifecycle-facade`; prior reviewed head `7bcd1c8658da95119ab725f454955d57f89f8e4f`, new head `cd29d0f...`; no new branch, no new PR).** **Pre-edit gate:** `origin/main` unchanged at `6f539b237019945443afe6156f9fc2a9fe32ffa4` (not advanced — no reconciliation required); `gh pr view 304` confirmed `OPEN`, head `9fdfb91739464809d442a9b8d14fa236adfaf624` (matched local/origin exactly, no divergence), `mergeable: MERGEABLE`; review threads re-checked — 6 comments total, all 3 original findings already replied-to/resolved in R2, zero new threads. **Blocking defect:** `redactForAnonymization` read `ImagingStudy.legalHold` and checked it in memory, but its `updateMany` write predicate never included `legalHold` — a hold acquired after the read but before the write had no effect on the write (TOCTOU race), letting a now-held image be silently redacted. **Fix:** the mutation predicate now is `{ id: imageId, clinicId, study: { clinicId, legalHold: false } }` — the check and the write are now the same database statement, closing the gap. **Zero-row classification** uses only a second, fully tenant-scoped `findOwnedImage` follow-up (never an unscoped-by-id lookup): not found under this tenant → `ImagingNotFoundError`; already redacted by a concurrent writer → benign no-op; otherwise (row exists, this tenant, not yet redacted) → `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`, since of the four predicate fields only `study.legalHold` can independently change between read and write. **No cross-tenant leakage:** a cross-tenant `imageId` is rejected by the initial ownership read before the legal-hold branch is ever reached, so it can never surface `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError` — directly tested. **Idempotency/legal-hold precedence:** re-read F2-PREP-009 — it does not specify this ordering; this task's chosen (pre-existing, re-verified correct) behavior keeps the read-time legal-hold check ahead of the already-redacted short-circuit, so an already-redacted-but-now-held row still throws `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError` rather than silently no-op-succeeding. **Deterministic concurrency regression test** added (`server/src/tests/imagingLifecycleFacade.test.ts` §8b): a new test-only synchronization seam (`__setRedactionPreMutationBarrierForTest`, a bare `() => Promise<void>` with no `clinicId`/`imageId` access, so it cannot itself alter authorization behavior) pauses an in-flight `redactForAnonymization` call immediately before its `updateMany`, via promise-based gating (`await barrierReached`), never a fixed-duration sleep; legal hold is then acquired concurrently and the gate released — asserts `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`, `originalName` unchanged, and an unrelated cross-tenant row untouched. Plus 3 more direct tests: a predicate-capture test proving `study.legalHold: false` is actually in the `updateMany` call's `where` clause (not only the in-memory check), a cross-tenant-held-image test (must be `ImagingNotFoundError`, never `ImagingLegalHoldViolationError`), and a denormalized-clinic-mismatch-under-legal-hold test. **Test seam review:** both `__setImagingStorageExistenceCheckerForTest` (pre-existing) and the new barrier seam retain Option B (module-private variable + narrow test-only setter) with full, precisely-scoped evidence written directly into `public.ts`'s doc comments — sequential single-process test execution, process-local state, every override `finally`-restored, zero production importers, cannot alter tenant predicates, and an explicit statement that this claim is scoped to sequential execution only, not general parallel-test safety. No parameter added to any accepted method. **Validation:** server `npx prisma generate && tsc --noEmit` exit `0`; `git diff --check` clean; `Imaging-Lifecycle-Facade` suite **34/34 passing** (was 30; +4 net, exactly the new §8b tests, zero regressions) on a real disposable PostgreSQL (`npm run test:runtime:postgres`, run `20260803T170803Z-37686cc0-46840`) — full `server:test:disposable-db` aggregate exit `0`, `cleanup.success: true`, zero residual Docker resources; `test:imaging-characterization`'s `CT-32` sub-suite independently re-confirmed **153/153**; `test:imaging` (direct, no disposable DB) **103/103**; `test:patient-privacy` (direct, no disposable DB) **38/38**; zero production callers re-confirmed for both the module and the new test seam (`grep`, excluding `*.test.ts`, zero matches outside the definition site). No `server/prisma/schema.prisma`, migration, route, or `.github/workflows/*.yml` file touched. **Windows Bridge CI:** the `Windows Bridge .NET tests` job (`BridgeOrchestratorTests.Heartbeat_AfterPairing_TransitionsToOnlineAndOmitsNullCapabilities`, `Assert.Contains` against an empty captured string) failed on this task's own fresh push (`cd29d0f`, run `30835968634`, attempt 1) — the identical failure signature seen on the prior R2 push (§27.16); zero `windows-bridge/` files touched by this PR at any point in its history; the `windows-bridge-pr` workflow triggers only on PR events and never runs against `main`, so no comparable control run exists. Classified **`FLAKY_UNVERIFIED`** (not `PR_CAUSED` — zero file overlap; not confirmable as `PRE_EXISTING_REPRODUCIBLE_ON_MAIN` — no control run obtainable; the defining flaky signature — identical commit, rerun flips the result — was independently confirmed on the R2 push and is being re-tested on this push). A rerun was triggered (`gh run rerun --failed`, mirroring established practice, not a code change) — final result recorded in this task's own delivery report and PR #304's final CI state; this document is not silently re-edited for status that becomes known after this line was written — any correction is its own new, additive entry. **Task status, CI-conditional:** if the final head's required checks are all green, `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TESTS_PASSED` / `REVIEW_THREADS_RESOLVED` / `PR_CI_PASSED` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_PROGRAM_REVIEW`; otherwise `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `TARGETED_TESTS_PASSED` / `CI_LAYERS_PASSED` / `PR_CHECKS_FAILED_UNRELATED_BLOCKER` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_PROGRAM_REVIEW` — see the delivery report for which applies. Merged: no. Deployed: no. Production verified: no. Full evidence: `docs/program/evidence/F2-IMPL-001-A_ADDITIVE_UNUSED_INTERNAL_IMAGING_FACADE_SKELETON.md` §28, JSON companion `r3AtomicLegalHold`. **Exact next action:** program-owner review of this R3 correction alongside R2; if the Windows Bridge job's disposition requires it, a separate, unrelated `.NET` test-flakiness investigation task is recommended, not created or authorized here; Stage 2/Stage 3 remain not started, not authorized by this entry.)

Prior update: 2026-08-02 (**F1-003-B2-R1 — Admission-Point Lock Closure, Current-Main Reconciliation, Remote CI Verification, and Evidence Sanitization (continued on the existing open branch `fix/f1-003-b2-retention-run-lock-race` / PR #280 — no new branch, no new PR, not merged, not rebased, not force-pushed).** **Main reconciliation:** fetched `origin/main` (`e8e05f669f049c4895e0c9a7f95db6f3fbd463fe`, PR #277 merge commit), confirmed both PR #276 (`0de9a04a...`) and PR #277 (`e8e05f66...`) merge commits are ancestors of `origin/main`, `git merge --no-ff origin/main` — **zero conflicts**, only 4 new additive F2-PREP-001/003 evidence files introduced, no schema/migration change; merge commit `2fc2d9b1985a33125d14251bca6077c149f589c4`; post-merge `git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main HEAD` confirmed true. **Residual race closed:** F1-003-B2's own evidence had explicitly documented one remaining gap — the live-run route still read `getPlatformSetting` (policy/runtime-toggle) *before* `acquireJobLock`, so a sufficiently delayed second concurrent request could in principle still reach admission after the first had already released it. This task moves lock acquisition ahead of the policy read entirely, making admission the very first variable-latency DB operation a live attempt performs. Both the winner and the lock-contention loser still read policy afterward, purely to build accurate audit metadata (never as part of the admission decision); a policy-gate rejection now releases the briefly-held lock and preserves the exact pre-existing `403`/audit shape; a policy-lookup failure after acquisition releases the lock and fails safely with a sanitized `500` and no fabricated audit row. **Route refactored into an exported factory** (`createDataRetentionRunHandler`, `server/src/routes/platformAdmin.ts`) so tests can inject a controllable `getPlatformSetting`; production always uses the real one. `releaseJobLock` (`jobLock.ts`) now logs — previously fully silent — when its own underlying DB release update genuinely fails; documented and tested as a deliberate trade-off (never rejects, so an already-completed cleanup's success is never retroactively reported as a failure; the lock is honestly left held until TTL expiry, not silently cleared). **Deterministic concurrency proof (not timing-luck):** a new test fires two concurrent live requests through a `getPlatformSetting` override that signals via a controllable `Deferred` the instant it is entered (proving admission already resolved) then blocks on another `Deferred` (never a `setTimeout`) until the test releases it; while both racers are deliberately parked mid-policy-lookup, the test directly queries the `JobLock` row and confirms exactly one is held — admission is fully decided *before either policy lookup is allowed to complete* — then releases both barriers and confirms the final result is still exactly `[200, 409]`. **4 new tests** (deterministic admission proof; policy disabled after lock acquisition — `403`, no cleanup, lock immediately reusable; policy lookup throws after lock acquisition — no cleanup, lock released, sanitized `500`, no fabricated audit row; `releaseJobLock()` genuine-DB-failure trade-off) — `retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts` **25→29 assertions**, run **6 consecutive times, 29/29 every time**. Prior update: 2026-08-02 (**F1-003-B2 — Retention Manual/Scheduled Run Mutual-Exclusion Race (blocking production-behavior defect, discovered by F1-003-P3's Layer 5 compatibility CI on PR #268 — PR #268 left completely open, unmodified, and blocked by this task; fresh isolated worktree/branch `fix/f1-003-b2-retention-run-lock-race`, execution baseline `origin/main` @ `70b1690c1a656c95cead7b42812cc9ae6447bfb7`, not the F1-003-P3 worktree, zero intervening commits).** **Authoritative failure evidence:** PR #268 run `30542271302`, job `ci-layers / Layer 5: full-suite/compatibility fail-safe (backend, legacy server:test DB-required members)` — `server/src/tests/retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts`'s concurrent-manual-runs assertion failed with `actual=[200,200]` vs `expected=[200,409]` (`Toplam: 20 ✓ 19 ✗ 1`); the immediately-following scheduled/manual-collision test in the same run passed. **Root cause proved before any fix was written:** `server/src/utils/jobLock.ts`'s `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` primitive (shared by the manual route and the scheduled cron, identical lock key `'data-retention-cleanup'`, `JobLock.name` genuinely the Prisma `@id` primary key per its own migration SQL) is atomic and race-free — empirically stress-tested with 130 concurrent-acquisition trials against a real disposable PostgreSQL container using the exact production Prisma 7.8.0 + `@prisma/adapter-pg` driver-adapter stack, **0 double-grants**. The actual defect was structural ordering in `server/src/routes/platformAdmin.ts`'s `POST /privacy/data-retention/run`: the shared lock was acquired only *after* two other variable-latency DB round trips (`getPlatformSetting` policy-gate read, `recordManualRunStarted` durable audit-write transaction) that both racers perform independently; since live-run cleanup against a small dataset completes in single-digit milliseconds, a fast racer's entire acquire→cleanup→release cycle could finish before a simultaneously-`Promise.all`-fired second racer even reached its own lock attempt, letting both legitimately (sequentially, not concurrently) acquire the lock and both return `200` — never a flaw in the lock's own atomicity, a genuine application-contract violation, not a test-scheduling assumption. **Fix (smallest correction, no new locking subsystem, no schema/migration change):** `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` exported from `jobLock.ts` (previously private, `withJobLock` itself untouched and still used unchanged by the scheduled cron and every other job in the codebase); the manual route's live-run branch now acquires the lock **first** — before either audit write — with both the winner and the loser still writing their own `manual_run_started` row (the existing, already-accepted audit-shape contract unchanged), the loser branching immediately to `manual_run_blocked`/`concurrent_run_in_progress` + `409` with zero cleanup invoked; a new edge case (lock acquired, then the started-write itself fails) now releases the lock before returning `500`, preventing a newly-possible 2-hour TTL orphan; cleanup wrapped in `try/finally` releasing on success, throw, or any other exit. **Tests: 5 new + 1 strengthened in `retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts` (20→25 assertions)** — a 10-consecutive-round in-test race gate (real PostgreSQL, genuine `Promise.all` concurrency, not simulated), 3 new tests exercising `acquireJobLock`/`releaseJobLock` directly (4-way concurrent grant, release-then-reacquire, throw-then-reacquire), 1 new test for the lock-acquired-then-started-write-fails edge case, and the existing scheduled/manual-collision test strengthened with a seeded-row-untouched assertion. **Post-fix: `test:retention-manual-run-audit` run 8 consecutive times, 25/25 every time (80 total race-gate rounds embedded, 0 double-successes, 0 double-blocks) — combined with the isolated primitive stress test, 210+ total concurrent-acquisition trials, zero races.** Also passing: `npm run typecheck` (0 errors), `npm run test:data-retention` (42/42, unaffected — exercises the cleanup function via injected deps, not the route), `npm run server:test:non-disposable` (exit `0`, 0 failure markers), root `npm run test:runtime:postgres` (exit `0`, cleanup succeeded, zero residual `com.noramedi.test-runtime=true` Docker containers/networks after every run — this task's own manually-provisioned repro container was also removed at session end, confirmed empty), `git diff --check` clean. **`server:test:legacy-db-required` does not exist on `origin/main`** (defined only inside PR #268's not-merged `ci-layers.yml`) — per instruction, the P3 CI aggregate was not copied onto this branch; the exact failing test was instead run directly against a disposable PostgreSQL provisioned the same way F1-003-P2's accepted runtime provisions one. **No schema/migration file touched, no production access, no `_prisma_migrations` change, no rollback performed** (a single `git revert` fully restores pre-fix behavior). **Lock scope: global/job-type-scoped** (one row for `'data-retention-cleanup'`, not tenant-scoped) — correct because a single live retention-cleanup pass spans all tenants in one run, confirmed by the unmodified no-cross-tenant-leakage test. **KVKK impact:** closes a real (never observed to cause actual double-deletion — the shared lock always prevented true simultaneous overlap) concurrent-manual-trigger risk; audit trail fully preserved for every branch including both new edge cases. **Crash/connection-loss behavior:** relies on the pre-existing, unchanged 2-hour TTL lease-expiry mechanism; not independently re-verified by this task, no crash guarantee claimed beyond what is directly tested. Files changed: `server/src/utils/jobLock.ts`, `server/src/routes/platformAdmin.ts`, `server/src/tests/retentionManualRunAudit.test.ts`, [evidence/F1-003-B2_RETENTION_RUN_MUTUAL_EXCLUSION_RACE.md](evidence/F1-003-B2_RETENTION_RUN_MUTUAL_EXCLUSION_RACE.md) (new), [evidence/F1-003-B2_retention_run_mutual_exclusion_race.json](evidence/F1-003-B2_retention_run_mutual_exclusion_race.json) (new), this file, `NORAMEDI_MASTER_TRACKER.md`, `phases/F1_CI_AND_TEST_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `evidence/README.md`. **PR #268 (F1-003-P3) was not modified, not merged, not closed by this task — it remains open and blocked**, referenced only for its own discovery-lineage evidence (run `30542271302`). Faz durumu remains `IN_PROGRESS`; F1's exit gate NOT satisfied; parent F1-003 not fully complete (P1/B1 merged; P2 `AGENT_COMPLETED`/PR #260 open; P3 `AGENT_COMPLETED`/PR #268 open and now additionally blocked pending this fix; P4 not started); **R-070 `OPEN`, R-046 `OPEN`, R-071 unchanged; G1/G2 remain `NOT_APPROVED`; KVKK physical/architecture freeze `ACTIVE`; KVKK baseline-stable declaration NOT GRANTED.** **Task status: agent completed yes; root cause proved via direct empirical stress-testing of the lock primitive (not assumed) yes; fix implemented yes; 5 new + 1 strengthened test yes (25/25); 8-consecutive-run/80-round race gate yes, zero double-success yes; full regression suite passed yes; merged no; deployed no; production verified no — `AGENT_COMPLETED` / `PR_OPENED_AWAITING_REVIEW`.** **Exact next task: after this bugfix PR is reviewed and merged, return to PR #268 for F1-003-P3-R2 main reconciliation and full-green CI closure** — not started, not authorized to start by this entry.)

No rebase, no force push, no reset, no `--ours`/`--theirs`. **Root-caused a real GitHub Actions-only defect, found only because this task actually inspected the original P3 PR's remote run rather than assuming it passed:** run `30536185063` (head `e99dd096d2d6d1222c9f7c7f59c0735ab4380979`) had **completed with overall conclusion `failure`** — `ci-layers / Layer 2: non-disposable backend tests` failed with `SyntaxError: The requested module '@prisma/client' does not provide an export named 'PrismaClient'`. Root cause: that job ran `npm ci` in `server/` then directly invoked `npm run server:test:non-disposable` without ever running `npx prisma generate`; `server/package.json` has no `postinstall` hook for this; Layers 3/4 succeeded on the very same run only because the F1-003-P2 orchestrator's `runMigrations()` always generates the client itself before any test script — a perfect natural experiment confirming the hypothesis. every from-scratch CI checkout hits it. **Fix:** added an explicit `npx prisma generate` step to `non-disposable-backend-tests` in `ci-layers.yml`, before the test-aggregate step — no test/application/schema file touched. **Closed the backend full-suite/compatibility gap the original P3 task's own evidence had left open and mis-estimated:** re-inspected `server/package.json`, `F1-003-P1_test_execution_contract.json`, and the legacy `server:test` chain programmatically (not by assertion) — legacy `server:test`'s 77-member chain contains **exactly 23** scripts (not the original P3 evidence's "roughly 14") absent from all three existing safe aggregates (`server:test:non-disposable` 68, `server:test:disposable-db` 9, `server:test:storage-integration` 1); all 23 confirmed `postgresRequired:true`/`minioRequired:false` per the P1 contract's own per-script fields (exact names: `test:auth`, `test:instagram`, `test:overlap-safety`, `test:availability-service`, `test:public-booking`, `test:public-booking-slots`, `test:notice-evidence`, `test:payments-list-field-scope`, `test:kvkk-lifecycle`, `test:clinic-bulk-export`, `test:security-incidents`, `test:communication-consent`, `test:communication-consent-backfill`, `test:communication-consent-reconciliation-report`, `test:communication-consent-reconciliation`, `test:communication-consent-audit-report`, `test:communication-consent-matrix-route`, `test:legacy-consent-correction`, `test:kvkk-high007-high008-schema-integrity`, `test:messages-consent-gate`, `test:recall-consent-gate`, `test:messaging-connection-scope`, `test:retention-manual-run-audit`). **Closure design, chosen to satisfy the smallest-additive/no-second-implementation/no-legacy-behavior-change constraints simultaneously:** one new, additive `server/package.json` aggregate, `server:test:legacy-db-required` (23 members, original legacy-chain relative order preserved; legacy `server:test` itself not read or modified); one new, additive orchestrator profile, `postgres-compat` (`scripts/test-runtime/lib/profiles.ts`'s new pure `resolvePostgresOnlyTestScript()`, unit-tested; `orchestrator.ts`'s `RunOptions.profile` widened; the existing `if (opts.profile === 'storage')` MinIO-provisioning branch is unaffected, so `postgres-compat` automatically gets PostgreSQL-only provisioning identical to `postgres` — same guard, same digest-pinned image, same migration path, same fail-fatal cleanup, same collision-resistant naming);
